“...no…”

“Then what is it—exactly—that makes you believe I will jeopardize my career and reputation for your vanity project?”

Lena gulps and hopes it helps keep her voice steady. “You’ll be protected, I promise.”

“You can’t realistically promise that,” Dr. Châtelet snaps with a sharp shake of her head, “and I can’t realistically give you the confidence you’re demanding. It is too soon. Either give me a better reason or wait.”

“I’ll give you a raise.”

“That’s expected.”

“A promotion.”

“I run this lab already.”

“I’ll fire you if you don’t.”

“Obsidian North would be happy to have me.”

Lena grits her teeth. The band of Kara’s watch cuts into her wrist, and her hand sweats around her phone that still hasn’t vibrated with a new message.

She just needs to do something.

“I’ll do anything,” she finally says.

“Anything?”

“Yes.”

Dr. Châtelet smirks and kicks a lab stool out to Lena. “Do the work.”

 

 

 

It takes about 15 minutes for Lena to actually work instead of pester Dr. Châtelet with, apparently, redundant and irritating questions.

“You said you’d do delivery,” Dr. Châtelet reminds her with a dismissive flick of her hand. “Go through the latest updates and leave me alone. It’s not like I’ll tell you anything new.”

Lena feels herself truly settle when she eventually gets a text from Winn of three emojis: a pigeon, an airplane, and a woman dancing in a red dress which Lena assumes is symbolic of the cape. She sends back a simple “thank you,” then almost puts her phone back on the desk before texting Eve permission to send hourly updates of anything important.

The first hour, a client shows up for his appointment and berates Eve for the lack of notice even after she shows him the cancellation email. Lena makes a note to hire an ergonomic consultant because the stool wreaks havoc on her posture. 

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The second hour, L-Corp’s stock closes 1.7% higher than yesterday. Dr. Châtelet shoots down Lena’s first delivery method proposal. 

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The third hour, Eve reports that there is nothing to report. Dr. Châtelet shoots down Lena’s second and third delivery proposals. 

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The fourth hour, quality fails five new technologies, which significantly delays two projects. Lena presents her third proposal again, but with more research, and earns Dr. Châtelet’s permission to proceed. 

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The fifth hour, Jess and Joey stop by Lena’s office and announce that there are only a couple more rounds of treatment remaining. Lena finds a new chair and shoves the old one out of the lab for good measure. 

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The sixth hour, Eve offers to put in a food order before she leaves. Lena and Dr. Châtelet agree on Thai.

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The seventh hour, Lena’s proposed carrier molecule passes L-Corp’s virtual simulations. Dr. Châtelet tasks her with making it.

Kara’s ribbon doesn’t come back.

The eighth hour, Lena tells Dr. Châtelet to go home because she’s perfectly capable of working alone in a lab. Dr. Châtelet states that Lena can’t be trusted without adult supervision.

And Kara’s ribbon comes back.

Any retort Lena might have fired off fizzles out with the rest of the adrenaline that sustained her for the last eight hours. She falls back into her chair, closes her eyes, and pinches the bridge of her nose as she breathes in deeply.

“It’s fine. I’m going to head out anyway.” Her phone vibrates and dings on the desk with a quick succession of texts, and she doesn’t bother looking at the sender before silencing the ringer and turning off the screen. “Did today change your stance on a human trial sooner rather than later?”

Dr. Châtelet yawns. “No, but it’s an interesting idea to have the contacts medicated. Make the carrier molecule first.”

“Will do.”

Lena nods and walks out of the lab, then reads through the text notifications on her phone, still buzzing with new messages.

‘Hey sorry long day and I didn’t have service
 I can tell you about it tonight
 Are you hungry?
 I can get Chinese if you want
 But I’m buying! I feel bad for being gone so long
Yeah I’ll get Chinese
 Are you home yet?’

She clicks off her phone and enters the elevator with heavy steps.

At least Kara’s okay.

Lena uses the time spent going up to her office, gathering her things, and heading back down to the lobby to think. Kara’s texting like nothing’s wrong, so Lena shouldn’t have any reason to believe otherwise. It makes her frantic focus seem a little silly now, and the small pang of humility moves her to pull out her phone once more.

‘Not hungry, and I’m just leaving the office now. You can meet me at my place’

‘Ouch I’ll definitely get you potstickers #ultimatecomfortfood :)’

 

 

 

Lena decides to play it cool. Kara doesn’t need to know the full extent of Lena’s day, and she can easily sum it up as a morning of lawyers and an afternoon in the lab. Kara can tell her as much or as little as she wants, Lena will be supportive because that’s what girlfriends do, and then they’ll watch TV and sleep at an hour too late to be considered reasonable.

The plan flies out the balcony door when Lena opens it to let Supergirl into her apartment.

Kara kisses her cheek on her way in and says something that’s probably a greeting, but Lena can’t hear it through whatever emotion is making her hands shake. She hopes it’s anger, familiar enough to process and dispose of without much effort, but she knows there’s more to it. She watches the yellow ribbon track Kara’s movements through the living room to the kitchen, and she wants nothing more than to lasso it over Kara’s shoulders and pull it tight so Kara can never leave her again.

“You disappeared.”

It takes a silent moment of Kara staring at her from the kitchen for Lena to realize she said those words out loud. Kara’s shoulders slouch, and she drops the bags of food on the counter.

“Not exactly.”

“Where’d you go?”

“Maaldoria. It’s far.”

Lena clears her throat and crosses her arms over her chest. “A text would’ve been nice.”

“I know. I’m sorry.” Kara abandons the food and strides over to Lena, but she stops at Lena’s flinch when she gets within arm’s reach. “Everything happened so fast. Someone asked me to find her daughter, then I was teleported to another galaxy and captured by slavers because Mon-El never listens and I didn’t have my powers, but everybody helped us break out and now we don’t need to worry about Roulette any more! And Alex is going to clear Izzy to return to her mom tomorrow morning, probably.”

Kara stares at her again, chest slightly heaving, and shifts her weight between her feet. Lena’s eyes sting from being opened so wide, but her hands stop shaking, and everything she could have been feeling begins to succumb to exhaustion as she reminds herself that Kara’s okay.

“That’s quite the adventure.”

“Are you mad?”

Lena coughs out a tired, disbelieving laugh. “How can I be? I think I just underestimated the superhero things. I thought I would be better, but I wasn’t ready for you to…” she exhales deeply and loosens her hands around her arms, almost motioning to her left wrist before catching herself and gesturing to her right instead, “to disappear.”

“If it helps, I don’t go off-planet much anymore,” Kara offers with a weak shrug.

“But you used to?”

“Kind of. I can tell you more, if you want.”

Lena shakes her head. “Not tonight.”

“Yeah, okay.” Kara shifts her weight again, and the movement of the cape behind her draws Lena’s attention faster than she would prefer. “Are you okay?”

“I will be.”

“Do you want me to leave?”

“No.”

That answer, at least, comes very easy to Lena. Kara must have caught it, too, because she hazards a step closer.

“Then, can I hug you?” Kara tentatively opens her arms. “Potstickers will definitely make you feel better, but I’d like to try this first.”

Lena smiles despite the exhaustion fighting to keep her lips in a frown. “Change first. I’m kind of mad at the cape.”

“Fair enough.”

 

 

 

Much to Winn’s delight, “dancing” becomes the euphemism for Supergirl activities. Kara makes a point to use it, too, when her heroics are too underground for NCPD’s Twitter but too dire for her to make it to dinner on time. It doesn’t really make Lena feel better, but it encourages her to spend more time in the ocular lab, and it’s absolutely worth it when she drops a binder full of her research on Dr. Châtelet’s desk.

“Let me know how confident you feel after reading this,” Lena says through a smirk. Dr. Châtelet scoffs but doesn’t look up from her computer.

“You weren’t told ‘no’ enough as a child.”

She waits for the elevator to head back to her office, and her phone buzzes with a Google alert of Livewire escaping from prison. She almost sends a text to Kara asking if she’ll be dancing tonight but ultimately deems it redundant. Last year, news about Livewire made it all the way to Metropolis, and there’s no chance Kara will let her stay loose for long.

“Lena!”

Lena looks up just in time to see Joey’s large smile before he runs past the half-open elevator doors and collides into her for a hug. She stumbles backwards with a grunt and strains to keep them both upright as her heels wobble under her ankles. “Seems like you’re doing better.”

“Oh my god! I’m so sorry, Ms. Luthor!”

Lena stifles a laugh at Jess’ panic and pats Joey’s head. “Don’t worry about it. I’m happy to see him, too.” Joey detaches himself but still grabs her hand and pulls her into the elevator. “Is everything going okay?”

“Yeah, we’re great.” Jess’ wide eyes dart between her brother and her boss, but Lena’s impressed by how well she keeps the fear out of her voice. “Mon-El brought Joey over so we could all grab lunch, but of course he went in the other elevator when it was already full, so of course we had to wait for the next one.”

Lena’s eyebrows scrunch together at the second mention of the unique name. “Who’s Mon-El?”

The elevator doors open again to the lobby in front of Lena’s office, and Lena finds “Mike” leaning casually over Eve’s desk while Eve types on her phone. They both look over at the sound of the elevator bell, and Eve promptly drops her phone in her bag.

“Ms. Luthor! Perfect! I was just letting you know I’m heading out for lunch. Do you need anything else before I leave?”

“I should be fine, thank you.”

“Then...” Mon-El drums his hands on top of Eve’s desk, and Lena just watches as the cornflower blue ribbon on her wrist brightens with the knowledge of his real name, “let’s eat!”

Eve and Jess roll their eyes, but Eve gathers her bag anyway and follows Mon-El to the elevator. Jess offers her hand to Joey, and Joey tightens his grip on Lena’s hand instead.

“Let’s go,” Jess says with only a twinge of impatience in her voice. “Ms. Luthor has a meeting in 20 minutes.”

Joey looks up at Lena, rainbow irises glimmering clear and radiant in the sunlight without a speck of black, and frowns. “You’re coming next week, right?”

Lena smiles, Joey’s lavender ribbon glowing stronger than ever on her wrist. “Wouldn’t miss it.”

Lena heads back to her apartment in time for the 3 PM news. There’s as much Livewire coverage as she expected, but it’s dull enough background noise while she reviews the more mundane reports of the day and occasionally checks her phone even though it doesn’t buzz. She just puts it down for the 27th time and moves to the kitchen when her balcony door slides open and Supergirl stomps in.

“Oh, perfect. I was about to…” Lena cuts herself off as she takes in the hero pacing in her living room, chest puffed and hands stiff on her hips. “What’s wrong?”

“Did you know?”

Lena frowns at the sharp tone. “I know many things. Care to specify?”

“Did you know James is Guardian?”

Lena’s breath catches. She knew this would be a fun conversation. “Yes.”

“Why didn’t you tell me?”

“Wasn’t my secret to tell.”

Kara groans and falls to the couch, hands rubbing furiously at her temples. “Someone should’ve told me.”

Lena taps her fingers on the counter. If the slouched shoulders and lack of laser eyes are any indication, Kara isn’t as angry as Lena thought she would be. At least that should make things easier. She rummages for a fresh bottle of wine and works on pouring herself a glass. “Did something happen?”

Kara groans again. “Leslie happened, and then…” she takes a deep breath, “and then James got hurt.”

Lena seamlessly grabs another glass and fills that as well. “I see.”

“He isn’t bulletproof or lightning-proof or anything proof!” Kara abruptly throws her arms into the air, and Lena awkwardly bends away lest she spill a single drop on her new rug or coffee table. “Why didn’t anyone tell me?”

“Probably because they knew you’d react like this.” Lena gives a playful smirk to Kara’s glare and offers one of the glasses of wine. “Here.”

“That doesn’t do anything for me.”

“I don’t know what else to contribute.” Lena moves the glass closer, and Kara eventually takes it with a sigh.

“That means you think I’m wrong.”

“I didn’t say that. James is as vulnerable as any other human and will never be as strong as you. He’s being reckless as hell.”

“But?”

Lena pauses and takes a long sip from her wine. “But… I didn’t think you would be so quick to turn away someone trying to help.”

“It’s not like that! He’s human!”

“So is Batman.”

“That’s different!”

“Okay,” Lena says with a hand up in surrender. “Fuck knows I don’t want to ruin my night defending Jimmy Olsen, but even I can acknowledge that he does good for the little people. That being said,” Lena continues louder over Kara’s attempted rebuttal, “Livewire was too much for him to handle, he wouldn’t stand a chance against any of the other big scary monsters you fight on a regular basis, and he definitely couldn’t do it in a skirt.”

“That’s the whole point,” Kara grumbles. She looks down at her wine and takes a suspicious sniff before grimacing.

“Please pretend to like it,” Lena asks with a playful nudge to Kara’s shoulder. “Colleague brought it down from Napa, and I’d love to give him a glowing review from Supergirl herself.”

Kara only gags on the first two sips of her drink. Lena tells her colleague she would love his wine recommendations for the next company party.

 

 

 

When Dr. Châtelet sends a calendar request for a meeting in 30 minutes, it takes all her restraint not to walk out on her lawyers and skip to the elevator. Still, she accepts it immediately, and she ushers her lawyers out early when they start their tired reminders of the trial being less than 24 hours away. She even escorts them to the elevator and suffers for 11 floors worth of lectures on how she should be prepared for anything because this is Lillian Luthor she’s dealing with. As soon as she’s walking alone, though, she almost forgets about it entirely, and the urge to skip the remainder of the way down the hallway returns. She pushes through the doors and strides inside the lab, but she patiently waits for Dr. Châtelet to finish typing and look up at her before smirking. The scientist breathes in, then shakes her head and snaps her laptop shut.

“In an alternate universe, this would never happen.”

“Good thing we’re in this universe instead.”

“You pushed this project to near-completion in a matter of days. I can’t find any evidence or run any simulations in which this will cause damage or side effects outside of the expected irritation and discomfort associated with regular eye drops or contacts. It will take almost immediate effect and metabolize through a human body safely. It is exactly the product you wanted to create, and I am confident that this will succeed in the next phase of trials.” Dr. Châtelet shrugs and releases a long, dramatic sigh. “Too bad I’ll be out of town for the next few weeks.”

Lena’s stomach sinks. “What?”

“I told you in December that it was better to travel at the end of January instead of during the holidays,” Dr. Châtelet explains. “My plans were pushed back due to forces outside of my control, so I’m traveling now.”

“Oh.”

“It’s unfortunate. I have to completely abandon this project until I’m back in the country. I’ll send you all my notes and the next steps for the project because I am a responsible employee, but I’m leaving my computer and all work related materials in the lab and will have absolutely no access to L-Corp resources or communication with you.”

“Okay…”

Dr. Châtelet stares at her hard, and Lena doesn't dare move a single muscle. “I am not leaving the completed, medicated contacts out in the open for anybody to take because that would be foolish. I usually keep them in the same part of the lab where we have been conducting all the tests, but I have an impeccable memory and dedication to L-Corp, so I would definitely put them somewhere safer if I were to be gone for a prolonged period of time. It’s a shame that all the ocular lab techs are going on vacation, too. Otherwise, I could trust that they would protect the contacts if I happened to leave them somewhere careless.”

“Definitely a shame,” Lena agrees slowly, eyes widening and movements cautious. “Of course, I could always take a look around once you leave just to be safe.”

“Absolutely not. I completely forbid it,” Dr. Châtelet says with a nod of her head. “You are not to enter this lab without my express permission even though your master credentials would override any security I could impose on this lab or my workstation, including the surveillance cameras and personnel entry logs.”

They both fall silent, but there could be explosions in the room and Lena wouldn’t be able to hear them over the word soon thundering in her mind. It takes several calming breaths and even more conscious reminders to stay professional, but Lena eventually holds out her hand, and her face relaxes into a smile.

“Can’t wait for you to leave.”

“Can’t wait to be gone.” Dr Châtelet shakes her hand firmly, but her reciprocal smile is soft, and Lena would swear she winked. “Good luck in the trial. Hope your alibi is as good as mine.”

 

 

 

Chapter End Notes

I asked Wifey if this story qualified as a slow burn, and she asked if I meant between LenaxKara or LenaxChatelet. Idk if I have a good answer to that.

TWO MORE CHAPTERS TO GO

 

refractallize.tumblr.com

Ascend

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

'Are you almost done???’

Lena smiles at her phone. Truthfully, she spent the majority of the afternoon antsy as hell without doing much work beyond attending meetings that she really couldn’t miss. Dr Châtelet’s final project status email, complete with her travel dates, didn’t help Lena’s productivity at all, and she practically sped through her last meeting in record time.

‘Just finished the last meeting for the day. Why?’

‘Come over!
 Are you okay with an early dinner?’

‘Sure’

‘Yay!!!!!!!
Wait that was too fast
Did you skip lunch again?’

‘Maybe’

 

 

 

Lena’s pretty sure she can smell food (and potentially smoke) from Kara’s apartment before she even enters the building. She shouldn’t have expected anything different considering the string of horrified emojis Kara sent in response to Lena’s last text, so she uses the elevator ride to brace herself for Kara’s puppy dog eyes and at least two full plates of dinner followed by a hefty dessert. The smoke doesn’t get better the closer she gets to Kara’s apartment, and after she knocks, she hears a muffled “crap” and a gust of wind before a breathless Kara opens the door.

“Hi, sorry,” Kara says through a strained, apologetic smile. “It’s an ice cream night again.”

Lena smirks. “Figured something like that.” She takes two steps inside and pauses. Kara’s apartment, usually bright and lively with the TV playing the sitcom of the hour, is dim and quiet save for the soft acoustic music from Kara’s laptop. Most of the light comes from the rays of the setting sun filtering through the open windows, the living room lamps off and the kitchen mason jar lights at half intensity over a spread of salmon and stir fry. A vase of plumerias sits in the middle of the dining table illuminated only by candles casting flickering light on the red rose petals scattered over a crisp, dark tablecloth.

“What’s all this?” Lena turns back to Kara, now holding out a stuffed teddy bear carrying a small, heart-shaped box and three fabric roses.

“Happy Early Valentine’s Day!” Kara grins, and it only gets wider as Lena slowly takes the bear. “I thought it might be a nice surprise before all the stuff happening tomorrow.”

Lena smiles. “It is, thank you. A recreation of the first time I came over, down to the burned dessert?”

“Okay, that part was supposed to be an improvement, not an imitation.” Kara leads Lena to the dining table and pulls out a chair. “...but I’m glad you noticed.”

The dinner is just as amazing as before, but Kara adamantly refuses to let Lena help with any clean up. She ushers Lena to the bedroom with stern directions to change into comfy clothes and open Mr. Bear’s box for a pre-dessert. Lena reluctantly complies, but her resistance instantly disappears when she finds her favorite of Kara’s t-shirts and sweats laid out among more rose petals on the bed. She changes quickly, and she makes sure to grab Mr. Bear on her way to the couch where Kara has already laid out DVD’s on the coffee table.

“I know you don’t really like romcoms, so I have My Cousin Vinny because I know it’s your favorite, but I thought it might be a bit too real for tomorrow,” Kara says with a shrug.

“You mean ‘inspirational.’ I can watch Mona Lisa Vito own her shit any day of the week.” Lena pops open Mr. Bear’s box, and a package of conversation hearts falls out. “You know, I’m not really nervous.”

“Really?”

“Yeah.”

Kara plops down on the couch and shakes her head at Lena offering one of the candy hearts. “You’re braver than I am. I was going to say you have nothing to worry about since we’ll be there to support you, but it’s good to know that you’re already okay.”

“Might be more nerve-wracking to know all of you will be watching.”

“No way!” Kara wraps her arm over Lena’s shoulders and pulls her into a half-hug. “You’ll do great, but Alex and Winn and I still want to be there.”

Lena snorts. “How did you bribe Alex?”

“She volunteered.”

“Seriously?”

“Yup, and it only took one pout.”

Lena laughs and settles against Kara’s side, attention now on the opening credits. Kara moves them until Lena’s back rests more on Kara’s chest, and Lena slowly sinks into Kara’s embrace. “Thank you.”

“Of course. I l—” Kara’s voice catches, and her fingertips twitch around Lena’s shoulder. “I mean, you’re welcome.”

Lena’s muscles swiftly reject the relaxation and stiffen again. She can’t tell if the heartbeat resounding throughout her body is her’s or Kara’s or their’s , and she desperately wants to take hold of Kara’s left hand and find out. Instead, she swallows hard and wills herself to calm down, starting with loosening her death grip on the candy hearts nearly crushed in her palm. She briefly examines them for damage, then smiles.

She supposes they’re called “conversation hearts” for good reason.

She pulls out the next heart with her left hand, a yellow one reading ‘I <3 you’ that she almost can’t read through the brightness of Kara’s bond, and holds it up in front of Kara’s face behind her without looking back. She keeps her eyes determinedly fixed on the television, not daring to move anything else when she could swear that Kara hasn’t even taken another breath. She concentrates on her heart still pounding painfully against her sternum and counts the beats because, surely, that’s the only accurate measurement for frozen time. She makes it up to five, now her absolute least favorite number, before she starts to lower her hand only for Kara to bite the heart out of her fingers. Lena breathes out, and it affirms her suspicion that Kara wasn’t breathing either when Kara exhales just as deeply at the same time. Lena smiles again, then scoffs at the spit remaining on her fingertips and wipes it off on Kara’s arm.

“So elegant.”

 

 

 

Despite Kara’s insistence that a Kryptonian is the best security that money can’t buy, Lena hires a professional bodyguard for the day, complete with a dark suit, sunglasses, and earpiece. He escorts her through the crowd of ravenous reporters with relative ease, and Lena can’t help but compare the scene to a rook protecting its queen on her way to the checkmate. She plays the part accordingly with her head high, not even sparing a glance down at the yellow, navy, and chartreuse ribbons glowing bright from her wrists, and focuses solely on the lawyers and their questions. She almost doesn’t acknowledge Lillian at all, but she can’t resist shooting her a raised eyebrow when she’s finally dismissed from the stand.

A camera flashes at the exchange, and Lena just hopes it caught her good side. If that’s the last picture ever captured of her with Lillian—Lena reveling in her win and Lillian’s hands shackled to the table—she might frame it.

It’s just as hectic outside when they leave, but the reporters’ new questions affirm that Lena’s testimony was all but damning Lillian to a life in prison. It’s only when her bodyguard drives them out of sight of the courthouse that Lena slumps into the stiff leather of the backseat with a deep breath, pulls off her stilettos, and laughs. It’s certainly not funny, and she’s not entirely looking forward to catching the media’s analysis of her testimony, but she feels so undeniably good .

She always did take a bit of a sadistic satisfaction in Luthor victories.

Lena spends the rest of the day at L-Corp for appearances more than anything else. Nobody mentions the trial to her face, but Jess gives her a smile she might describe as “proud,” and Eve messages her after every call she receives from somebody wanting to talk to her. The trial didn’t garner as much airtime as she thought it would, but the coverage of her testimony almost seems… positive? She watches a full segment on one of the 24-hour news channels with her eyes wide, and she would’ve believed it too good to be true until Morgan Edge reminds everyone of the blood on the Luthor name. She sighs and reaches for the remote, but another analyst speaks up before she can mute them:

“Need I remind you that she never testified in any of Lex’s trials?”

“Probably didn’t want to incriminate herself.”

“She never testified, which means she was never subpoenaed, either. Both the prosecution and defense had plenty of reason to try to pin something on her. If the highest paid law firms in the country couldn’t make a case for her involvement—not even something completely circumstantial for when they were living under the same roof—I’m inclined to believe that it doesn’t exist. The past two years and today’s performance in court have shown that dialogue on Lena Luthor will only be relevant when Lex is not part of the conversation.”

Lena stares at the screens until she receives a message from Eve regarding the latest attempt of Lillian’s lawyers to contact her. She mutes the television, directs Eve to forward everything to voicemail until next week, fixes her mascara in the reflection of her laptop, then returns to work.

Kara respects Lena’s wishes to be left alone for the day without as much as a fly-by for lunch, but she shows up promptly at 4:30 PM and nearly crushes Lena in a strong hug. If it weren’t for Dr. Châtelet’s instructions to leave the infrared contacts immersed in solution for at least 48 hours, Lena probably would’ve dragged Kara down to the lab. Instead, she leads her to the company car waiting outside, shuffles into the backseat, and rolls up the partition behind the driver. Kara delicately places a hand on Lena’s knee and releases a sympathetic sigh.

“Was it as awful as it looked?” 

“Did it really look that bad? I almost had fun.” 

“I mean, I definitely wouldn’t have wanted to do it.”

“Well, I don’t want to do it again, but if it means keeping Lillian in jail, then it’s worth it. Could do without the twelve calls from her lawyers, though.”

Kara reaches for Lena’s right hand and squeezes gently. “What do you think she wants?”

Lena snorts. “I don’t know, and I don’t care. Thinking of going to tomorrow’s court session since she wants to see me so bad, though.”

“Do you want to go together?”

“I have something in the morning, but I can meet you there.” Lena smiles at Kara squeezing her hand again, then looks down at the floating white bonds on her wrist. “You know, I’ve kind of always hated Lillian’s ribbon.”

Kara adjusts her glasses and looks at the partition before returning her attention to Lena. “What color is she?”

“Amber, but, even on Lex, it never got very bright. It was like it had holes or shadows in it.”

“That doesn’t seem great.”

“It’s not.” Lena pauses, then rubs her thumb over Kara’s knuckles, still watching the white bonds with Lillian’s amber nowhere to be seen. “We fought a lot when I was younger, but I still tried to earn her approval. I could obviously never compete with Lex, but my father introduced her as my ‘new mother,’ so I was doing my duty as her ‘new daughter.’ At some point, I think I realized that my true mother would always shine brighter than my new mother, and I stopped caring as much.” Lena shifts in her seat and rests her head on Kara’s shoulder. “My magic isn’t the most impressive in the universe, but I don’t know if I’d like who I would be without it.”

“Would you really be that different?”

“Maybe. I’d probably be more humble.” She grimaces in mock disgust and relaxes into a smile again when Kara laughs softly. “It reminded me that I was my mother’s daughter. I was always a Luthor, but I was also always better.” Lena’s left hand twitches at her side. “Or at least, I always had a reason to be better.”

 

 

 

Lena wakes up alone in Kara’s apartment, bed cold and windows wide open to the dark, early morning. Her phone doesn’t have any notifications, but there’s a Post-It with a simple, scribbled heart stuck on her favorite mug by the coffee maker. She breathes easier after that, starts the coffee, and leisurely goes through the motions of getting ready for the day in an attempt to leave as late as possible. Kara still hasn’t shown up ten minutes later than Lena should have left, but, really, throwing out perfectly warm coffee is blasphemy. Lena takes her drink to the high dining table with every intent to savor it like it deserves, but she pauses when she sees another Post-It next to two keys on the table in front of her usual chair.

‘Lock up when you leave?’

She reads the note six times and carefully sounds out the syllables in her head just to make sure she still has a decent grasp on the English language before she trusts her interpretation of the message. She gingerly reaches out for the keys, and the first contact of her skin on solid, cool, real metal completes the circuit and spurs her to action. She turns off the lights, fastens the new additions to her key ring, triple-checks the clasp of her watch, grabs her bag, bolts out of the apartment, and clumsily locks the door from the outside fast enough to give The Blur a run for his money.

After all, the sooner she finishes her work, the sooner she can bring Kara to the ocular lab.

 

 

 

Linda and Otto assure Lena that the serum test is really just a formality. All of Joey’s images came back clean of masses, and, as he would proudly and excitedly attest, he feels better than ever.

So, naturally, it turns into a celebration.

Lena holds herself back from buying flowers and balloons with the rationale that a child Joey’s age wouldn’t really know what to do with either of them, but the Plastinos must not have had the same opinion. Both doctors hug her upon arrival and usher her into the room packed with decorations all while chattering with the siblings and catching up on their weeks. She can barely see Jess through the mylar balloons even though they only stand on opposite sides of the hospital bed, and she offers to help take the large bouquets of flowers home before going back to the office.

Once the tourniquet comes out, though, anticipation weighs down the jovial atmosphere into something quiet and solemn. Lena only manages to conquer the new gravity when Joey flinches under Linda’s touch. Her hand moves of its own accord to rest on Joey’s shoulder, and he looks up at her with beautifully clear, colorful eyes crinkled at the corners from his smile. When Linda finishes, Otto takes the sample to another room, but it does little to lighten the mood. Linda talks while cleaning and bandaging Joey’s forearm, idle things about the microscope Otto splurged the last of their grant money on at the end of the year that Lena engages with just to keep conversation flowing for the next agonizing fifteen minutes, and Jess silently runs her hand through Joey’s shaggy hair.

Then, the door creaks open, and Otto steps through with a small stack of papers and a grin on his face.

“All clear.”

He fires a confetti popper hidden behind the papers, setting off a loud bang only rivaled by the volume of Jess’ squeal. Lena suddenly becomes grateful for the bulky decorations stuffed in the room when she realizes nobody saw her jump at the noise, and she watches as Jess keeps Joey locked in a tight hug.

Linda approaches Lena and looks up at her with a small smile, faint green circle around her irises. “None of this would have happened without you.”

Lena shakes her head. “I just funded it. It really wouldn’t have happened without you and Otto. I don’t think I ever properly thanked both of you for the fake isotope, either.”

“You have done more than enough.”

“Then I don’t need to also fund a group trip for ice cream?”

“I didn’t say that.”

 

 

 

The Plastinos confirm that they’ll meet Lena and the siblings at the parlor downtown, then help load the bouquets into Jess and Lena’s arms. Joey helps lead them out of the incubator space, and they all assure the professors that they’ll be fine walking to Lena’s car.

“If you keep fussing,” Lena points out, “the ice cream might melt by the time we get there.”

“Yeah, and then it would be ice soup!” Joey chimes in beside her.

Jess hums. “Isn’t that just water?”

“No, it’s soup because of the milk and chocolate chips.”

“What if it doesn’t have chocolate chips?”

“Then it’s ice milk.”

Lena snorts and nods goodbye to the professors before following Joey and Jess to the crosswalk in front of the parking lot. They take three steps onto the street when Lena pauses at the sound of a car horn blaring and getting closer from a car hurtling down the road straight towards them. 

She drops the flowers from her arms and yanks the siblings back to the sidewalk just in time to make room for Supergirl landing hard in the middle of the crosswalk with her arms outstretched in front of her.

Supergirl shouts something, but Lena can’t discern the words over the loudening car horn followed by the crash of the car hood under the hero’s palms. The tires squeal on the street in a cloud of smoke and burned rubber even when confronted with Supergirl’s unyielding hands. Lena can barely make out the face of the driver, pale skin stretched thin around their wide eyes. Supergirl shouts something else at the driver, but the driver just frantically shakes their head and raises their hands. Supergirl’s heels dig into the asphalt, and she drops to one knee before placing a hand under the car and lifting up, raising the front half of the vehicle into the air. With the tires off the ground, Lena can finally hear Supergirl telling the driver to turn the car off, only for the driver to claim they can’t.

“Shift to neutral!” Lena yells over the still-revving engine. “Neutral! Then turn it off!”

Both Supergirl and the driver look over to her, and Lena repeats her instructions until the driver nods. Eventually, the gears shift with a sickening grind, and the engine switches off. Lena exhales deeply, then turns back to Jess and Joey.

“Are you okay?”

“Yeah, just…” Jess murmurs, gaze still transfixed on Supergirl. “Wow.”

“She’ll probably come say ‘hi.’”

“Ah! Oh my god. Really?”

Jess practically throws the flowers into Joey’s arms, her nervous babbling drowning out his protests, and smooths out her skirt. Lena shakes her head with a smile, then resumes watching Supergirl and the driver. The car is now (with the help of Kryptonian strength, Lena assumes) safely parked along the sidewalk, and Supergirl finishes up a hug with the driver before half-jogging to Lena. Lena’s mouth pulls to something between a smile and a smirk, and she repeats Supergirl Supergirl Supergirl in her mind a few times to remind herself not to slip up just in time to stop the hero from picking up the flowers still in the street.

“Don’t worry about it,” Lena quickly intervenes. “There are plenty to spare. Trust me. And somebody wants to meet you.”

Supergirl bounces lightly on her feet. “I love this part.”

Lena barely restrains from rolling her eyes before gesturing to the siblings, Jess now frozen in place staring at Supergirl and Joey calmly holding the flowers. “This is Joey—”

“Hi.” Supergirl bends down to Joey’s level with a smile. “Do you want help with those?”

“It’s okay.”

“Oh. Well, obviously you’re super strong, so I can see how they’re not a problem for you.”

“They’re not heavy. They’re flowers.”

“—and this is Jess,” Lena quickly interjects.

To her credit, Supergirl seems unfazed as she stands back up and offers a handshake to Jess. “Hi. Nice to meet you.”

Lena thinks she can see a hint of blush at the tips of Jess’ ears, likely less covered in make-up than the rest of her skin. Jess sputters out some syllables, some of which Lena believes could be English, and impressively keeps up a minimal conversation with Supergirl as Lena herds them to her car, carefully looking both ways before stepping onto the street.

“You know,” she can’t help saying over her shoulder, “we’re going to get ice cream after this. Would Supergirl be willing to share any of her perks?”

“Sure. I can probably get Supergirl-sized scoops for everybody if I ask nicely.”

“Oh my gosh, no, we couldn’t impose—” Jess stammers.

“Sounds good.” Lena pops open the trunk, and Joey places the flowers inside before Supergirl can try to help.

“Good job, buddy.” The hero holds her hand up for a high-five. “Are you okay with getting ice cream with Supergirl?”

Joey stares at her hand, then looks up at Lena. “Does that mean I can sit up front with you?”

Supergirl’s smile falters, and her fingers haltingly wither and loosely curl back into her palm. Lena laughs and nods, and Joey runs around to the front passenger seat.

“Please don’t be too offended, Supergirl,” Jess squeaks. “Ms. Luthor is Joey’s hero.”

Lena’s laugh cuts short. She opens her mouth to say one of her practiced dismissals in response to corporate praise, but no words can escape around the sudden lump in her throat.

A Luthor… a hero?

“Can’t blame him.” Supergirl answers easily with a small smile in Lena’s direction. “She’s pretty great.”

 

 

 

Lena wishes she had the foresight to open her camera app when they meet the Plastinos at the ice cream parlor with no warning about their unexpected guest. She thought Otto was going to faint when he opened the car door to the cape and crest, and he probably would’ve fallen backwards if it weren’t for Linda’s hand supporting his back. Overall, though, Lena considers the outing a success, especially when Jess finally figures out how to say a complete, coherent sentence to Supergirl.

All progress flies out the window when Supergirl hugs her goodbye and zooms off into the sky, but Lena thinks it still counts for something.

Kara Danvers coincidentally shows up around the corner approximately 45 seconds later, and Lena walks with her to the Cayenne when Jess adequately convinces her that she and Joey will be okay getting home with the professors. When they get out of earshot, Lena turns to Kara and gives her a playful glare.

“You are never driving my car again.”

“In my defense,” Kara says through a sigh, “I was kind of busy actually stopping a literal ton of metal from running over my girlfriend to remember how to regularly stop a runaway car.”

“Does that mean it would be safer if you drove from the outside?”

Kara groans until Lena takes her hand and assures her that she’s joking. It doesn’t stop Lena from guarding her keys a little tighter or walking a bit faster to the driver’s seat, though, but it most likely helps shorten Kara’s pout from the regular eight seconds to only two.

There’s a significantly thinner crowd outside the courthouse than yesterday, and only two reporters manage to slip past Kara’s defenses to accost Lena on their way inside. They find seats without incident, but Lena’s heart rate picks up a bit when she spies Kara’s blue-gray ribbon swiveling to her side.

“Hey.” Alex sits to Lena’s right, sandwiching her between the two sisters.

Lena’s eyebrows scrunch together briefly. “Hey?”

“Everything okay?” Kara asks from Lena’s other side.

Alex’s sharp eyes survey the courtroom, pausing only on Lillian being led into the courtroom by the bailiff. “Maggie got a tip that Lena visited Lillian last night.”

Lena scoffs. “Bullshit.”

“She was with me all night,” Kara adds.

“There’s video footage. Winn’s helping her look into it.”

Lena opens her mouth again to comment on the absurdity of her wanting to see her stepmother when the bailiff invites everyone to rise for the judge. Her first instinct is to leave. If somebody is targeting her, then she should get out of the same room as them, right? Even if they’re in handcuffs? However, there are advantages to staying in public spaces, too, like directly disproving any falsified video “evidence” of wrong-doing. Plus, Alex would have warned her sooner if there were signs of an immediate threat. It could just be an elaborate smear campaign to negate all the positive press from yesterday. But who else would go through the trouble other than Lillian?

They sit back down, and Kara takes Lena’s hand in a firm hold. The yellow bond flashes bright out of the corner of Lena’s eye, but she doesn’t chance looking at Kara. Instead, she finds her dull amber bond and mentally accuses it of everything she can think of.

Of course, it gives away nothing.

Lena squeezes Kara’s hand back.

Corben takes the stand, and Lena’s only a little glad that she’s far enough away that she can’t reach far enough to punch the sneer off of his face.

“Mr. Corben,” the prosecutor starts, “will you tell the court how you know Lillian Luthor?”

Corben straightens up in his seat. “She’s the doctor who saved my life.”

“By turning you into a weaponized cyborg?”

“Objection,” Lillian’s lawyer interrupts. “Calls for speculation.”

The judge nods. “Objection is sustained.”

“Okay, we’ll move on. After Lillian Luthor saved you, did she order you to work for Cadmus?”

“She didn’t have to order me to do anything. I believe aliens should be wiped from the Earth, starting with Supergirl and her cousin.”

“And what, if anything, did Lillian Luthor tell you about the Medusa project?”

“Earth is in crisis. Aliens threaten our safety and way of life, bringing violence and disease. She wanted to save the human race.”

The prosecutor takes a deep breath. “That’s enough. Thank you.”

“It’s not enough!” Corben emphatically continues, shifting forward in his seat. “Lillian Luthor is not a criminal. She wanted to save you until Supergirl silenced her. This trial is a conspiracy to condemn the one person who wants to save your lives. Your children’s lives!”

A ripple of dissent spreads throughout the spectators, and Lena shifts uncomfortably in her seat. Alex lightly touches her arm.

“Do you want to leave?”

Lena can’t form an answer before the banging of the gavel and the judge’s call for order echoes throughout the chamber.

“Settle down,” the judge commands. “We must have order in the courtroom. Counselor? Get control over your witness.”

“Mr. Corben has become antagonistic and clearly is not willing to answer the questions as directed. Permission to treat the witness as hostile, Your Honor?”

Corben slowly rises from the stand, and Alex and Kara’s grips on Lena tighten.

“Allow me to treat you as hostile,” Corben growls.

Lena crashes to her knees as Alex and Kara both pull her to the ground. A beam of kryptonite-green flashes overhead, and the rest of the audience disperses from the room in a flurry of screams and panic. Lena scrambles behind Alex and Kara to cover behind a partition in front of the first row of spectator seats, and she flinches at each additional blast in the room. Alex catches her attention and shakes her head, then holds up a finger to her lips. Lena nods, but the reminder doesn’t stop her eyes from widening at seeing Lillian and Corben’s legs stride behind Alex and out of the room.

At the sound of the door slamming closed, Lena finally takes a breath, and her head whips around to look at Kara. Kara’s chest heaves as she sits with her back to the partition, but her expression is determined, and Lena’s heart jumps into her throat when Kara focuses that resolute gaze on her. All Lena’s mind can see is green, not the yellow flashing at her side that intensifies when Lena clings onto Kara’s hand nor the cerulean blue of Kara’s eyes muddied by the shadow from her glasses.

A scream pierces through the room from the outside, and Lena releases Kara’s hand.

We can’t live in fear.

“Go,” Alex hisses.

Kara nods to both of them, then rips off her glasses and disappears from the room. Alex stands up almost as quickly, and she helps Lena to her feet.

“Stay close. We’ll go slow since everybody’s outside.”

“To where?”

“My car. We can’t trust that nobody is waiting by yours right now.”

The courthouse is, as Alex surmised, empty, and it makes following Alex fairly easy despite her high heels. They turn sharply out of the building, but Lena catches a glance of the plaza in front of the courthouse, two lines of scorch marks etched into the concrete. Alex ushers her to the sidewalk and walks closely to her side instead, one hand guiding Lena forward and the other reaching up to her earpiece.

“I need agents downtown. Now!”

Alex might have said something else, but Lena doesn’t hear it over the shattering glass from the skyscraper across the street victim to a piece of a massive tower crane. She walks faster, distantly remembering Alex’s direction to move slow, only to be jerked backward by Alex’s hand on her shirt. 

The cyborg from the Medusa attack stands imposingly in the middle of the sidewalk, mechanical eye burning with a white hot heat.

Alex is halfway to covering Lena’s body with her own when a motorcycle screeches to a stop in front of them and a shield blocks the beam of heat vision. Guardian grunts under the force of the attack, but he still manages to step off his bike and push back against the onslaught of heat.

“Call Supergirl!” Alex yells, now drawing her gun from her hip.

Lena reaches for the watch on her wrist but doesn’t get the chance to flip open the top before a white unmarked van pulls up next to them. Corben, shirt torn and a green glow emanating from a mass in his chest, pops out of the sliding side door.

“It’s time to go, Miss Luthor.”

Alex moves between Lena and Corben with her gun drawn. “Not with you.”

Corben smirks. “How about with him?”

The cyborg speeds up to Alex and snatches her outstretched arm before wrenching it back and throwing her to the side. Lena screams Alex’s name, and Guardian clambers up from his defeated heap on the ground to catch her before she lands on the cement. Lena breathes out a short-lived sigh of relief until the cyborg pushes her roughly into the van and slams the door shut. Corben grabs her hands and zip ties them together, then uses them to hold her up as the van accelerates away from the scene.

“Happy to have you join us,” Lillian quips from the driver’s seat. “Please make yourself comfortable.”

Lena glares at Lillian through the rearview mirror while Corben pats her down. He quickly finds her phone and crumples it in his hand.

“Sorry, no phone calls allowed.” Lillian peers into her side mirror, then sighs. “Metallo, can you please stop the uninvited guest from crashing our party? 6 o’clock.”

Corben continues to hold Lena in place, but she starts to kick at his shins when she sees her yellow left-ribbon brightening and growing longer, anything to delay Corben from opening the door. He just shrugs, then casually tosses her towards the front of the van and throws open the door.

“No!” Lena screams.

From around Corben’s silhouette, all she can see is the sickening green flash before her yellow ribbon snips short and flush around her wrist.

 

 

 

Chapter End Notes

Shine

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Lena almost wishes she had been blindfolded. It could’ve saved her from the torment of avoiding eye contact with Corben (Metallo, Lena corrects herself) in the back of the van. She spends most of the silent car ride staring out the rear windows and trying not to think about the zip tie biting into the thin, tender skin high on her wrists. Kara’s yellow ribbon hasn’t changed at all since the showdown at the courthouse, and Lena tells herself it’s because Winn didn’t think to put a damn tracker in the watch that doesn’t need to be manually activated.

It’s definitely not because Kara took a blast of kryptonite point blank.

Maybe it’s for the better. Lena doesn’t know anything about Lillian’s larger plans, and she still doesn’t believe that Lillian would really harm her. Breaking out prematurely wouldn’t accomplish anything.

So, Lena just needs to wait and observe.

They stop at a freeway underpass devoid of traffic signs and transfer to a different unmarked van, black with windowless sides and back windows tinted too dark to see anything outside. She can’t see anything out the front window either without holding her neck at an uncomfortably awkward angle, making her resolution to “wait and observe” fairly difficult. Metallo takes over driving (for which Lena is infinitely grateful) and Lillian sits in the front passenger seat with a tablet in her lap. It becomes abundantly clear that neither of them will spare Lena any attention when they hit a hard bump and don’t react to Lena’s grunt of pain.

If she can’t observe, maybe she can get them to talk.

“Is this going to be a long drive?” she asks with a huff. “Might as well sleep if it’s going to be this entertaining.”

Lillian taps her tablet and hums. “Maybe another hour.”

“Where are we going?”

“Just a little family road trip.”

“You could’ve gotten a better Lex impersonator. He’s not even bald.”

The van noticeably accelerates, and the next bump in the road nearly sends Lena flying out of her seat. Lillian just sighs.

“Acting out won’t get you any attention, Lena. We’ll get there soon enough.”

If the sunset is any indication, the drive takes much longer than an hour, but Lena doesn’t waste her breath trying to get more information. Eventually, the van slows down as they approach a mountain, and Lillian taps a button on her tablet to open a large metal door at the base. Metallo rolls in slowly into a massive concrete bunker, desolate save for the sporadic rows of 18-wheelers and shipping crates. Lena doesn’t make any move to leave the van after he parks, but he slides open the door and fixes her with an expectant, crazed stare that makes her rise to her feet. He lifts more than assists her out of the van, and she stumbles in her heels as she lands on the ground.

“Honestly, Lena,” Lillian tsks, “you could’ve chosen better shoes.”

“Sorry, ‘kidnapping’ wasn’t on my calendar. Give me a warning next time.”

Lillian tilts her head curiously to the side. “I sent a video.”

“Didn’t get to me fast enough. What is all this?”

Lillian smirks and nods to Metallo, then begins walking to the center of the facility with Lena and Metallo close behind. “One of Lex’s facilities. A giant satellite receiver embedded in the top of this mountain monitors outer space for signs of intelligent life.”

“To warn of an alien invasion?” Metallo chimes in.

“Bingo! Because we know more are coming.”

“Which makes me glad I joined the cause.”

Lillian smiles sweetly at her lap dog, and Lena swallows down her vomit.

“Lex kept enough rations to sustain us here for a long time if we need it. He also kept an arsenal, vaults around the world loaded with all the things he’d collected to level the playing field and defeat Superman once and for all, and one of those vaults is right here.” Lillian stops at a metal stand topped with a thin box engraved with the image of a handprint and a glowing red light at the bottom, then presses buttons on her tablet until a robotic voice announces that it’s ready for authorization. Lillian turns back to Lena with a taut smile. “All we need is to open it.”

Lena stares incredulously at Lillian, then at the box. “It’s a biometric lock.”

“It scans for Luthor DNA.”

“Tell me you don’t have a bit of Lex frozen somewhere.”

“I didn’t say Lex’s DNA,” Lillian sighs. “I said Luthor. Any Luthor can open it, and you are part of that family tree.”

Lena releases a dry bark of a laugh. “So that’s why you need me. Does it kill you to know I’m the bastard who’s more of a Luthor than you’ll ever be?”

Her next quip cuts off into a gasp as strong hands grip her wrists and forcefully pry them apart, easily snapping the zip tie holding them together.

“I think you should listen to Ms. Luthor,” a gruff voice growls behind her. He twists out her arm until her palm faces the lock pad, then walks her forward despite her squirming and efforts to fight back. “I’m trying to play nice.”

“He doesn’t want to hurt you, Lena,” Lillian adds. “Put your hand on the pad and we can all start getting along again. I promise.”

Lena tries once more to break free of his grasp to no avail. “No.”

With a final yank, Lena loses her balance and falls palm first onto the lock. Something sharp pricks into her fingertips, and the light at the bottom of the pad turns green. The floor in the middle of the facility sinks in, then slides open to reveal another level of metal. A fortified enclosure rises from underground with thick metal beams and cage grating encapsulating its contents. Once fully risen, lights illuminate the objects inside, most prominently the encased Lexosuit displayed in the center.

“See?” Lillian says through a saccharine smile. “That wasn’t so bad, was it?”

Lena flexes her hand and gulps, then follows Lillian, Metallo, and the cyborg to the enclosure.

“Well, this should make you feel safe.” Lillian doesn’t break stride as the doors to the cage smoothly slide open. “Lex’s warsuit, an Atomic Axe, a Black Mercy… everything we need to rid this planet of Kryptonians and every other alien invader once and for all.”

Lena surveys the interior, and her gaze momentarily locks on the Lexosuit. Her skin raises into goosebumps, the vivid memory of Lex explaining his development of synthetic kryptonite just three years ago flashing in her mind’s eye, and her hand wanders to an open case of sonic grenades. It’s not entirely the same design as when they supported the defense against Zod’s invasion, but it’s similar enough that her fingers wrap around the bomb with more familiarity than she would prefer. Her stomach sinks with the realization that she’ll probably never know the full extent of her unwilling contribution to his arsenal.

Her left wrist suddenly feels heavy at her side with the weight of the watch. How could she call Kara here? Lillian doesn’t have an engineering background extensive enough to construct all the weapons, but Lex surely taught her how to use them, especially if she helped fund the project. This vault (and every other vault Lex built) is the most dangerous place in the world for Kara.

Metallo picks up a suitcase and carries it out of the enclosure.

What if these weapons make it outside the vault?

Heart racing, Lena sets the grenade back into its foam-padded case. “You’re worse than Lex.”

Lillian saunters up to Lena and pulls the grenade back out. “I promise you: I will be on the right side of history. Maybe then you’ll believe that I was looking out for your best interests after all.” She delicately closes the lid of the case, and then turns to Lena with a face of—if Lena didn’t know any better—humble sincerity. “Whether you like it or not, we will always be family.”

Lillian turns and hands the case of grenades to the cyborg, then pauses and moves to something else in the enclosure.

 

 

 

Lena raises her left hand and reaches for the crown of the watch only to see her yellow ribbon already zooming down from the sky.

 

 

 

She spins around as Kara smashes through the ceiling, and her feet immediately begin moving her forward, but Lillian manages to cut ahead.

“Supergirl! You’re just in time,” Lillian announces. “I’ve been wondering if these worked.”

Lillian lobs the sonic grenade to Kara, and Kara catches it easily. Lena’s shout to warn her about the weapon turns into empty air when the cyborg comes up behind her once more and traps her between his arms. Kara holds the grenade for less than a second before dropping it, falling to her knees, and covering her ears with a cry of pain.

“Well, that was easy.” Lillian watches Supergirl with a smirk. “You want to try more of these toys?”

Lena kicks at the cyborg's shins and struggles against his grip. “Don’t hurt her!”

“Sorry.” Lillian looks to Lena with that same, vile smile, but Lena would swear that her lips curled up a fraction more at the corners before her next words:

 

 

 

“Your girlfriend needs to pay for what her cousin did to your brother.”

 

 

 

Lena loses the breath in her lungs before the cyborg throws her to the ground. Her vision goes blurry at the smack of her head on concrete, and the ringing in her ears is only drowned out by her heartbeat thudding ‘she knows’ throughout her body. Feet shuffle past her, and Lena can’t say for certain if the subsequent explosions and crashes are from fighting in the bunker or from her blood vessels bursting with every feeble attempt to sit up. She struggles to keep a steady hold of her consciousness and focuses on counting the bangs echoing in the bunker or the objects in front of her or the repetitions of ‘she knows’ still coursing through her veins, but her body instantly relaxes at the touch of a hand landing lightly on her shoulder.

“Lena…” 

Everything fades to black.

 

 

 

She wakes to warmth and rushing wind and a pounding heartbeat. Her eyes slowly flutter open to a dark sky and a frantically flashing yellow.

“Kara?”

“Yeah, Lena.” Hands gripping under Lena’s legs and around her back tighten their hold. “It’s me. I’m here.”

“She knows.” Lena clutches the collar of Kara’s suit and huddles closer into Kara’s chest. “She knows who you are. Your identity.”

“It’s okay. You’re safe, and that’s what matters.”

“But you’re not safe. You got hurt. You’re…”

Lena’s next words get caught in the choked sob that she traps in her throat. Kara kisses her head, and Lena watches the bond for the remainder of the flight as she tries not to cry.

 

 

 

Kara flies them through the wide, glass door of a high building in the city and touches down just before striding through a hallway of windows and labs. Lena’s head throbs with every hard stomp to the point that she almost asks Kara to fly again when they turn sharply into a room where Alex fiddles with medical equipment next to a hospital bed.

“Alex, she hit the ground pretty hard—”

“Bed.”

Kara bends down and helps Lena onto the bed, and she supports Lena’s back to sit up as Alex shines a pen light into her eyes. Lena follows the pen when instructed and answers basic questions about her condition until Alex seems satisfied.

“You seem okay,” Alex concludes, “but you still might be concussed. I’d prefer if you stayed here for monitoring.”

Lena nods but quickly stops when it exacerbates her headache. Kara’s hand gently eases her down to lay on the bed, and she falls into a dreamless sleep.

 

 

 

She wakes again to warmth, this time in the form of a thick blanket and Kara’s hand loosely holding hers. She slowly looks to the side while holding her head still, not wanting to also wake the headache now reduced to a dull and distant throb, and finds Kara sleeping in a chair close to the bed with her head inelegantly rolled back over the top of the chair.

“She’d do anything for you, you know?”

Lena startles at the voice from her other side, but Alex just gives her a soft smile. Lena clears her throat.

“It’s mutual.”

“Good.” Alex approaches her with the pen light once again, and Lena silently goes through the motions unprompted. “Feeling better?”

“Much. Is everybody okay?”

Alex sighs. “It was a close call. I don’t know what would’ve happened if J’onn hadn’t flown out there.”

Lena takes in the new name and decides not to comment. “And James?”

“Yeah, he’s fine.”

“What happened with that video of me?”

“Winn restored the original footage and showed that it was Hank Henshaw, the cyborg. Maggie won’t let it see the light of day.” Alex steps back and crosses her arms. “You still seem okay. Can’t rule out concussion, though. You should take it easy for the next few weeks.”

“Okay.” Kara interrupts Lena with a loud snore, and Lena smiles. “What time is it?”

“About 3 AM. Kara brought you a change of clothes if you want them.”

Kara jolts up at the mention of her name. “I’m awake.”

“Obviously,” Alex deadpans. “She’s okay to leave.”

“Thanks.”

Alex nods with another smile, then quietly exits the room. Kara squeezes Lena’s left hand.

“Do you want me to take you back to your apartment?”

Lena looks down at their joined hands, yellow bond flashing bright, and knows the only place she wants to go right now.

“Can you take me to L-Corp?”

 

 

 

Chapter End Notes

refractallize.tumblr.com

Noesis

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Kara only agrees to take Lena to L-Corp once Lena explains that she wants to gather some things so she can work from home. They use the front entrance instead of Lena’s office balcony—Lena now in comfortable shoes and Kara changing into her civilian clothes once they touch down around the corner—and take the elevator to the top floor in silence. Lena quickly gathers some folders from her desk, but pauses when she reaches for her laptop.

Dr. Châtelet mentioned that the surveillance cameras would be on.

Hands shaking, she flips open the computer.

“That’s not ‘not working,’” Kara points out.

“I’ll be fast.” Lena accesses the security camera feed and sets it on loop for her office, the ocular lab, and elevator, then logs out and slams it shut before shoving it under arm. “Okay, let’s go.”

Kara half-jogs behind Lena back to the elevator, and Lena’s hand beats her to press the button for the 29th floor instead of the lobby.

“One more thing,” Lena murmurs.

“Am I gonna have to keep a close eye on you while you work from home?”

There’s a playful lilt to Kara’s voice, but Lena just shrugs and doesn’t take her eyes off the closed elevator doors. Kara takes Lena’s hand, and Lena squeezes tightly until she pulls Kara out when they reach their floor.

“Is everything okay?”

Lena’s hand trembles as she inputs her security code on the first set of locks to the ocular lab. “Yeah. Just checking on a project that I’d actually like your help with.”

“At three in the morning?”

“Ideally.”

Kara huffs behind her, but she doesn’t say anything else while they make their way through the second and third locked doors to Dr. Châtelet’s workspace. Lena finds the contact case easily, practically on display in front of a mirror and eye occluder on the desk, and takes a deep breath. The lab is colder than usual, and Lena suspects that Dr. Châtelet left the air conditioning on. The windowless space has only half of the overhead fluorescent lights on, and Lena’s sure she could make the room pitch black if she turned them off.

It’s perfect.

She slowly turns to Kara, contact case in hand. “You know how I said I don’t have a color?”

Kara nods.

“For Joey’s treatment,” Lena continues, “we used infrared light to eradicate the virus. One of the professors was sensitive to infrared, so we developed contacts to filter it out. When I tried it on, one of my ribbons changed color.” She takes another deep breath. “It suggested that the ribbons could radiate outside of the human visible spectrum. Maybe they’re not colors as much as they’re wavelengths. So, I thought maybe my wavelength could be something that I just wasn’t biologically built to see.

“These are contacts that should let me see infrared. I don’t know if they’ll work, but you know me better than anyone, so I figured you would be most likely to have a ribbon of me bright enough to give me the best shot of seeing my color. You don’t have to do anything special. Just kind of… stand there…”

“Absolutely,” Kara cuts in. “I’m happy to help.”

Lena hazards a small smile, then steps forward and kisses Kara soundly.

There’s no going back. If there’s any chance Kara will think she’s crazy, she might as well take everything she can get for now.

She washes her hands in one of the lab sinks before taking the chair in front of the desk and opening the contact case. “I’m only going to put one in,” she explains quietly, partially buying time to give the tremor in her hands to calm down. “Just to be safe, and I want to be able to compare it to my regular vision. We might need to turn the lights off because it will make me sensitive to all lights in general, but it also has a long-pass filter to limit higher frequency light from entering my eye. I’ll let you know.”

“Okay.”

Lena takes a final deep breath, then slides a lens onto her right eye.

The contact settles easily, and her sight on the right side is instantly reduced to the deepest of reds. Within seconds, her eye prickles and stings from what she assumes is the medicated solution permeating through to her retina. Lena closes both of her eyes as they begin to water and tilts her head up to try to retain as much solution as possible.

“Sorry. Not exactly attractive.”

“You’re fine,” Kara reassures. “Does it feel okay?”

“Stings a little as expected. Nothing to worry about.”

“Do you want the other lights off?”

“Not yet.”

“Okay.”

Lena hears Kara shift, then feels a hand softly rubbing her back. They stay there for a few minutes, Lena’s heart beating harder and louder with every second and every thought about how this could go wrong.

And then the stinging goes away.

She nods, and Kara’s hand disappears. She rises from the chair, not trusting her knees to reliably support her with how hard they shake, and lowers her head. 

She opens her eyes.

At first, the discrepancy of colors between her right and left eye disorients her. She scrambles for the occluder on the desk, then uses it to block her left eye. The deep reds from when she first put on the contact have gotten brighter, and she can make out auras of even darker reds around the overhead lights and computers in the room. Patches of the atmosphere appear red, too, and Lena would guess that those pockets of air are warmer than the air around them.

“Are the lights okay?” Kara asks from the side.

“Yeah. The filter works really well. All I can see is the heat. Doesn’t hurt at all.”

“That’s great! Ready to turn around?”

Lena laughs dryly. “No, but I will.”

“Whenever you’re ready.”

Lena counts to three, then slowly turns to Kara.

“Oh my god.”

A ribbon tied securely to Kara’s left wrist, unmistakable and strong, points directly at Lena.

More accurately, it points to Lena’s left wrist.

It’s unlike any other ribbon Lena has ever seen. The true color of the ribbon is indisputably dark, and Lena wouldn’t consider it red, but she hardly cares about qualifying it when the glow is much more captivating. She could only describe it as velvet, soft and hazy but solid, with a sheen that glistens with a fluidity like light shimmering on top of gently rippling water. Each glimmer shines with a different color—emerald, amethyst, coquelicot, amber, garnet, gold, silver, lavender, pink, navy, chartreuse, and so, so many more—faint enough to be invisible if not for Lena’s wavelength providing a backdrop for them all.

It’s absolutely beautiful.

Lena’s bottom lip quivers, and she nearly drops the occluder from how profusely her palms sweat. Kara takes a step forward, and the velvet ribbon shortens to stay at the midpoint between her and Lena. Lena uncovers her left eye and sees Kara’s yellow ribbon still shining bright and ending at the same midpoint.

Lena rushes forward before her legs can give out and takes a firm hold of Kara’s left hand.

“It’s here,” she whispers. “It’s really, really here.”

Kara grins. “That’s great!”

Lena brings Kara’s hand up to her lips and kisses it, then keeps it close to her face and squints with only her right eye open as best as she can.

The velvet ribbon pulses in time with the yellow ribbon.

She releases a disbelieving laugh and sniffs before kissing Kara’s hand again. “I can’t believe it.”

“I can,” Kara responds simply. “You’re a genius. But, Lena, why are you holding my left hand?”

Lena freezes, but her tears fall on their joined hands anyway. At the first drop, she jerks her head up to look Kara in the eyes. Although her vision is still disorienting from the long-pass filter, she can still make out Kara’s kind expression, open and without judgment.

“Soulmates.”

Kara’s eyebrows scrunch together. “What?”

Lena bites her lip and looks back down at their hands. “Left-ribbons are reserved for soulmates. I have you on my left wrist, too. I’m sorry. I didn’t want to tell you until I knew for sure because what if I wasn’t actually there and you thought—”

Kara ducks her head and silences Lena with a gentle kiss. Lena’s breath catches painfully in her chest, and she feels more tears rolling down her cheeks until Kara pulls away.

“That explains a lot,” Kara murmurs.

“What?”

“Yeah. Everything feels so right with you, and it’s just so intense. I didn’t question it, but I didn’t understand why I needed to do everything I could to be with you, and to protect you, and to…” Kara smiles softly.

 

 

 

“And to love you.”

 

 

 

Lena stares at Kara, and Kara reinforces her words with a nod.

 

 

 

The bonds flash bright, but Lena drops their hands so she can bring her arms around Kara’s shoulders and pull her close for another kiss.

 

 

 

“I love you, too.”

 

 

 

Chapter End Notes
